<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:

  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works

* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes

  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

## Is your feature/enhancement proposal related to a problem? Please describe.

<!--A clear and concise description of what the problem is. Ex. I'm always frustrated when [...]-->
I have some Cython codes (`.pyx` files) in my project, and I use setuptools backend (the only backend supports building C extensions AFAIK) to compile them.

For source installation, those `.pyx` files should be included in sdist, but setuptools doesn't include them by default.

The problem is, currently `pdm build` will always succeed to build the sdist and the wheel even if those `.pyx` files are not included in the sdist, and the created sdist cannot be installed due to the lack of `.pyx` files.

## Describe the solution you'd like

<!--A clear and concise description of what you ant to happen.-->

I suggest we first build the sdist, and then build the wheel from that sdist. This is the same approach as [build](https://pypi.org/project/build/).
</issue_description>

### 1.1-1.5 Problem Analysis

**Problem in clear terms**: 
The current PDM build process builds both sdist and wheel independently. This means if a project has Cython files (.pyx) that aren't properly included in the sdist, the build will still succeed, but the resulting sdist will be invalid (cannot be installed). The desired behavior is to build sdist first, then build the wheel from that sdist to ensure sdist validity.

**Steps to reproduce**:
1. Create a project with Cython code (.pyx files)
2. Use setuptools backend (which doesn't include .pyx files by default)
3. Run `pdm build`
4. Observe that both sdist and wheel are built successfully
5. Try to install from the sdist - it fails due to missing .pyx files

**Best practices for fixing**:
- Follow the same approach as the `build` package
- Make minimal changes to existing functionality
- Ensure backward compatibility
- Proper error handling when sdist is invalid

    31	It was originally built for personal use. If you feel you are going well

    10	from pdm.cli.options import (
    11	    config_setting_option,
    12	    no_isolation_option,
    13	    project_option,
    14	    skip_option,
    15	    verbose_option,
    16	)
    17	from pdm.exceptions import ProjectError
    18	from pdm.project import Project
    19	
    20	
    21	class Command(BaseCommand):
    22	    """Build artifacts for distribution"""
    23	
    24	    arguments = (verbose_option, project_option, no_isolation_option, skip_option, config_setting_option)
    25	
    26	    @staticmethod
    27	    def do_build(
    28	        project: Project,
    29	        sdist: bool = True,
    30	        wheel: bool = True,
    31	        dest: str = "dist",
    32	        clean: bool = True,
    33	        config_settings: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
    34	        hooks: HookManager | None = None,
    35	    ) -> None:
    36	        """Build artifacts for distribution."""
    37	        from pdm.builders import SdistBuilder, WheelBuilder
    38	
    39	        hooks = hooks or HookManager(project)
    40	        config_settings = project.core.state.config_settings
    41	
    42	        if project.is_global:
    43	            raise ProjectError("Not allowed to build based on the global project.")
    44	        if not project.is_distribution:  # pragma: no cover
    45	            raise ProjectError("tool.pdm.distribution must be `true` to be built.")
    46	        if not wheel and not sdist:
    47	            project.core.ui.echo("All artifacts are disabled, nothing to do.", err=True)
    48	            return
    49	        if not os.path.isabs(dest):
    50	            dest = project.root.joinpath(dest).as_posix()
    51	        if clean:
    52	            shutil.rmtree(dest, ignore_errors=True)
    53	        if not os.path.exists(dest):
    54	            os.makedirs(dest, exist_ok=True)
    55	        hooks.try_emit("pre_build", dest=dest, config_settings=config_settings)
    56	        artifacts: list[str] = []
    57	        with project.core.ui.logging("build"):
    58	            if sdist:
    59	                project.core.ui.echo("Building sdist...")
    60	                loc = SdistBuilder(project.root, project.environment).build(dest)
    61	                project.core.ui.echo(f"Built sdist at {loc}")
    62	                artifacts.append(loc)
    63	            if wheel:
    64	                project.core.ui.echo("Building wheel...")
    65	                loc = WheelBuilder(project.root, project.environment).build(dest)
    66	                project.core.ui.echo(f"Built wheel at {loc}")
    67	                artifacts.append(loc)
    68	        hooks.try_emit("post_build", artifacts=artifacts, config_settings=config_settings)
    69	
    70	    def add_arguments(self, parser: argparse.ArgumentParser) -> None:
    71	        parser.add_argument(
    72	            "--no-sdist",
    73	            dest="sdist",
    74	            default=True,

     5	from pdm.builders.base import EnvBuilder

     7	
     8	class WheelBuilder(EnvBuilder):
     9	    """Build wheel in isolated env with managed Python."""
    10	
    11	    def prepare_metadata(self, out_dir: str) -> str:
    12	        self.install(self._requires, shared=True)
    13	        requires = self._hook.get_requires_for_build_wheel(self.config_settings)
    14	        self.install(requires)
    15	        filename = self._hook.prepare_metadata_for_build_wheel(out_dir, self.config_settings)
    16	        return os.path.join(out_dir, filename)
    17	
    18	    def build(self, out_dir: str, metadata_directory: str | None = None) -> str:
    19	        self.install(self._requires, shared=True)
    20	        requires = self._hook.get_requires_for_build_wheel(self.config_settings)
    21	        self.install(requires)
    22	        filename = self._hook.build_wheel(out_dir, self.config_settings, metadata_directory)
    23	        return os.path.join(out_dir, filename)

     1	from __future__ import annotations
     2	
     3	import logging
     4	import os
     5	import shutil
     6	import subprocess
     7	import textwrap
     8	import threading
     9	from logging import Logger
    10	from pathlib import Path
    11	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, ClassVar, Iterable, cast
    12	
    13	from pyproject_hooks import BuildBackendHookCaller
    14	
    15	from pdm.compat import tomllib
    16	from pdm.environments import PythonEnvironment
    17	from pdm.exceptions import BuildError
    18	from pdm.models.in_process import get_sys_config_paths
    19	from pdm.models.requirements import Requirement, parse_requirement
    20	from pdm.models.working_set import WorkingSet
    21	from pdm.termui import logger

    23	if TYPE_CHECKING:
    24	    from pdm.environments import BaseEnvironment

    26	
    27	class LoggerWrapper(threading.Thread):
    28	    """
    29	    Read messages from a pipe and redirect them
    30	    to a logger (see python's logging module).
    31	    """
    32	
    33	    def __init__(self, logger: Logger, level: int) -> None:
    34	        super().__init__()
    35	        self.daemon = True
    36	
    37	        self.logger = logger
    38	        self.level = level
    39	
    40	        # create the pipe and reader
    41	        self.fd_read, self.fd_write = os.pipe()
    42	
    43	        self.start()
    44	        self._output_buffer: list[str] = []
    45	
    46	    def fileno(self) -> int:
    47	        return self.fd_write
    48	
    49	    @staticmethod
    50	    def remove_newline(msg: str) -> str:
    51	        return msg[:-1] if msg.endswith("\n") else msg
    52	
    53	    def run(self) -> None:
    54	        with os.fdopen(self.fd_read, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as reader:
    55	            for line in reader:
    56	                self._write(self.remove_newline(line))
    57	
    58	    def _write(self, message: str) -> None:
    59	        self.logger.log(self.level, message)
    60	        self._output_buffer.append(message)
    61	        if len(self._output_buffer) > 10:
    62	            self._output_buffer[:-10] = []
    63	
    64	    def stop(self) -> None:
    65	        os.close(self.fd_write)
    66	        self.join()
    67	
    68	
    69	def build_error(e: subprocess.CalledProcessError) -> BuildError:
    70	    """Get a build error with meaningful error message
    71	    from the subprocess output.
    72	    """
    73	    output = cast("list[str]", e.output)
    74	    errors: list[str] = []
    75	    if output and output[-1].strip().startswith("ModuleNotFoundError"):
    76	        package = output[-1].strip().split()[-1]
    77	        errors.append(
    78	            f"Module {package} is missing, please make sure it is specified in the "
    79	            "'build-system.requires' section. If it is not possible, "
    80	            "add it to the project and use '--no-isolation' option."
    81	        )
    82	    errors.extend(["Showing the last 10 lines of the build output:", *output])
    83	    error_message = "\n".join(errors)
    84	    return BuildError(f"Build backend raised error: {error_message}")
    85	
    86	
    87	def log_subprocessor(
    88	    cmd: list[str],
    89	    cwd: str | Path | None = None,
    90	    extra_environ: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    91	) -> None:
    92	    env = os.environ.copy()
    93	    if extra_environ:
    94	        env.update(extra_environ)
    95	    outstream = LoggerWrapper(logger, logging.INFO)
    96	    try:
    97	        subprocess.check_call(
    98	            cmd,
    99	            cwd=cwd,
   100	            env=env,
   101	            stdout=outstream.fileno(),
   102	            stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
   103	        )
   104	    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
   105	        e.output = outstream._output_buffer
   106	        raise build_error(e) from None
   107	    finally:
   108	        outstream.stop()
   109	
   110	
   111	class _Prefix:
   112	    def __init__(self, executable: str, shared: str, overlay: str) -> None:
   113	        self.bin_dirs: list[str] = []
   114	        self.lib_dirs: list[str] = []
   115	        for path in (overlay, shared):
   116	            paths = get_sys_config_paths(executable, vars={"base": path, "platbase": path}, kind="prefix")
   117	            self.bin_dirs.append(paths["scripts"])
   118	            self.lib_dirs.extend([paths["platlib"], paths["purelib"]])
   119	        self.site_dir = os.path.join(overlay, "site")
   120	        if os.path.isdir(self.site_dir):
   121	            # Clear existing site dir as .pyc may be cached.
   122	            shutil.rmtree(self.site_dir)
   123	        os.makedirs(self.site_dir)
   124	        with open(os.path.join(self.site_dir, "sitecustomize.py"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as fp:
   125	            fp.write(
   126	                textwrap.dedent(
   127	                    f"""
   128	                import sys, os, site
   129	
   130	                original_sys_path = sys.path[:]
   131	                known_paths = set()
   132	                site.addusersitepackages(known_paths)
   133	                site.addsitepackages(known_paths)
   134	                known_paths = {{os.path.normcase(p) for p in known_paths}}
   135	                original_sys_path = [
   136	                    p for p in original_sys_path
   137	                    if os.path.normcase(p) not in known_paths]
   138	                sys.path[:] = original_sys_path
   139	                for lib_path in {self.lib_dirs!r}:
   140	                    site.addsitedir(lib_path)
   141	                """
   142	                )
   143	            )
   144	        self.shared = shared
   145	        self.overlay = overlay
   146	
   147	
   148	class EnvBuilder:
   149	    """A simple PEP 517 builder for an isolated environment"""
   150	
   151	    DEFAULT_BACKEND: ClassVar[dict[str, Any]] = {
   152	        "build-backend": "setuptools.build_meta:__legacy__",
   153	        "requires": ["setuptools >= 40.8.0", "wheel"],
   154	    }
   155	
   156	    _shared_envs: ClassVar[dict[int, str]] = {}
   157	    _overlay_envs: ClassVar[dict[str, str]] = {}
   158	
   159	    if TYPE_CHECKING:
   160	        _hook: BuildBackendHookCaller
   161	        _requires: list[str]
   162	        _prefix: _Prefix
   163	
   164	    def get_shared_env(self, key: int) -> str:
   165	        if key in self._shared_envs:
   166	            logger.debug("Reusing shared build env: %s", self._shared_envs[key])
   167	            return self._shared_envs[key]
   168	        # We don't save the cache here, instead it will be done after the installation
   169	        # finished.
   170	        return self._env.project.core.create_temp_dir("-shared", "pdm-build-env-")
   171	
   172	    def get_overlay_env(self, key: str) -> str:
   173	        if key not in self._overlay_envs:
   174	            self._overlay_envs[key] = self._env.project.core.create_temp_dir("-overlay", "pdm-build-env-")
   175	        return self._overlay_envs[key]
   176	
   177	    def __init__(self, src_dir: str | Path, environment: BaseEnvironment) -> None:
   178	        """If isolated is True(default), the builder will set up a *clean* environment.
   179	        Otherwise, the environment of the host Python will be used.
   180	        """
   181	        self._env = environment
   182	        self.executable = self._env.interpreter.executable.as_posix()
   183	        self.src_dir = src_dir
   184	        self.isolated = environment.project.config["build_isolation"]
   185	        self.config_settings = environment.project.core.state.config_settings
   186	        mode = "Isolated" if self.isolated else "Non-isolated"
   187	        logger.info("Preparing environment(%s mode) for PEP 517 build...", mode)
   188	        try:
   189	            with open(os.path.join(src_dir, "pyproject.toml"), "rb") as f:
   190	                spec = tomllib.load(f)
   191	        except FileNotFoundError:
   192	            spec = {}
   193	        except Exception as e:
   194	            raise BuildError(e) from e
   195	        build_system = spec.get("build-system", self.DEFAULT_BACKEND)
   196	
   197	        if "build-backend" not in build_system:
   198	            build_system["build-backend"] = self.DEFAULT_BACKEND["build-backend"]
   199	
   200	        if "requires" not in build_system:
   201	            raise BuildError("Missing 'build-system.requires' in pyproject.toml")
   202	
   203	        self.init_build_system(build_system)
   204	
   205	    def init_build_system(self, build_system: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   206	        """Initialize the build system and requires list from the PEP 517 spec"""
   207	        self._hook = BuildBackendHookCaller(
   208	            self.src_dir,
   209	            build_system["build-backend"],
   210	            backend_path=build_system.get("backend-path"),
   211	            runner=self.subprocess_runner,
   212	            python_executable=self.executable,
   213	        )
   214	        self._requires = build_system["requires"]
   215	        self._prefix = _Prefix(
   216	            self.executable,
   217	            # Build backends with the same requires list share the cached base env.
   218	            shared=self.get_shared_env(hash(frozenset(self._requires))),
   219	            # Overlay envs are unique for each source to be built.
   220	            overlay=self.get_overlay_env(os.path.normcase(self.src_dir).rstrip("\\/")),
   221	        )
   222	
   223	    @property
   224	    def _env_vars(self) -> dict[str, str]:
   225	        paths = self._prefix.bin_dirs
   226	        if "PATH" in os.environ:
   227	            paths.append(os.getenv("PATH", ""))
   228	        env: dict[str, str] = {}
   229	        if self.isolated:
   230	            env.update(
   231	                {
   232	                    "PYTHONPATH": self._prefix.site_dir,
   233	                    "PYTHONNOUSERSITE": "1",
   234	                }
   235	            )
   236	        else:
   237	            env_paths = self._env.get_paths()
   238	            project_libs = env_paths["purelib"]
   239	            pythonpath = [*self._prefix.lib_dirs, project_libs]
   240	            if "PYTHONPATH" in os.environ:
   241	                pythonpath.append(os.getenv("PYTHONPATH", ""))
   242	            env.update(
   243	                PYTHONPATH=os.pathsep.join(pythonpath),
   244	            )
   245	            paths.append(env_paths["scripts"])
   246	        env["PATH"] = os.pathsep.join(paths)
   247	        return env
   248	
   249	    def subprocess_runner(
   250	        self,
   251	        cmd: list[str],
   252	        cwd: str | Path | None = None,
   253	        extra_environ: dict[str, str] | None = None,
   254	        isolated: bool = True,
   255	    ) -> None:
   256	        env = self._env_vars.copy() if isolated else {}
   257	        if extra_environ:
   258	            env.update(extra_environ)
   259	        return log_subprocessor(cmd, cwd, extra_environ=env)
   260	
   261	    def check_requirements(self, reqs: Iterable[str]) -> Iterable[Requirement]:
   262	        missing = set()
   263	        conflicting = set()
   264	        project_lib = self._env.get_paths()["purelib"]
   265	        libs = self._prefix.lib_dirs + ([project_lib] if not self.isolated else [])
   266	        if reqs:
   267	            ws = WorkingSet(libs)
   268	            marker_env = self._env.marker_environment
   269	            for req in reqs:
   270	                parsed_req = parse_requirement(req)
   271	                if parsed_req.marker and not parsed_req.marker.evaluate(marker_env):
   272	                    logger.debug(
   273	                        "Skipping requirement %s: mismatching marker %s",
   274	                        req,
   275	                        parsed_req.marker,
   276	                    )
   277	                    continue
   278	                if parsed_req.identify() not in ws:
   279	                    missing.add(parsed_req)
   280	                elif parsed_req.specifier and not parsed_req.specifier.contains(
   281	                    ws[parsed_req.identify()].version, prereleases=True
   282	                ):
   283	                    conflicting.add(req)
   284	        if conflicting:
   285	            raise BuildError(f"Conflicting requirements: {', '.join(conflicting)}")
   286	        return missing
   287	
   288	    def install(self, requirements: Iterable[str], shared: bool = False) -> None:
   289	        from pdm.installers.core import install_requirements
   290	
   291	        missing = list(self.check_requirements(requirements))
   292	        if not missing:
   293	            return
   294	        path = self._prefix.shared if shared else self._prefix.overlay
   295	        env = PythonEnvironment(self._env.project, python=str(self._env.interpreter.path), prefix=path)
   296	        install_requirements(missing, env)
   297	
   298	        if shared:
   299	            # The shared env is prepared and is safe to be cached now. This is to make
   300	            # sure no broken env is returned early when run in parallel mode.
   301	            key = hash(frozenset(requirements))
   302	            if key not in self._shared_envs:
   303	                self._shared_envs[key] = path
   304	
   305	    def prepare_metadata(self, out_dir: str) -> str:
   306	        """Prepare metadata and store in the out_dir.
   307	        Some backends doesn't provide that API, in that case the metadata will be
   308	        retrieved from the built result.
   309	        """
   310	        raise NotImplementedError("Should be implemented in subclass")
   311	
   312	    def build(self, out_dir: str, metadata_directory: str | None = None) -> str:
   313	        """Build and store the artifact in out_dir,
   314	        return the absolute path of the built result.
   315	        """
   316	        raise NotImplementedError("Should be implemented in subclass")
   317

"</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>def build_wheel(
        self,
        wheel_directory: str,
        config_settings: Optional[Mapping[str, Any]] = None,
        metadata_directory: Optional[str] = None,
    ) -> str:
        """Build a wheel from this project.

        :param wheel_directory: The directory to write the wheel to
        :param config_settings: The configuration settings for the build backend
        :param metadata_directory: The directory to reuse existing metadata from
        :returns:
            The name of the newly created wheel within ``wheel_directory``.

        .. admonition:: Interaction with fallback

            If the ``build_wheel`` hook was called in the fallback for
            :meth:`prepare_metadata_for_build_wheel`, the build backend would
            not be invoked. Instead, the previously built wheel will be copied
            to ``wheel_directory`` and the name of that file will be returned.
        """
        if metadata_directory is not None:
            metadata_directory = abspath(metadata_directory)
        return self._call_hook(
            "build_wheel",
            {
                "wheel_directory": abspath(wheel_directory),
                "config_settings": config_settings,
                "metadata_directory": metadata_directory,
            },
        )

Let me research this approach.<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the issue more carefully, I think the approach should be:

1. When both sdist and wheel are requested, first build the sdist
2. Then extract the sdist to a temporary directory
3. Build the wheel from that extracted sdist directory instead of the original source

This would ensure that if the sdist is missing files (like .pyx files), the wheel build would fail because those files wouldn't be available in the extracted sdist.

However, looking at the pyproject-hooks API, there's no direct "build_wheel_from_sdist" method. The standard approach according to PEP 517 is:
- build_sdist(directory) 
- build_wheel(directory, config_settings, metadata_directory)

But to build wheel from sdist, I would need to:
1. Call build_sdist() to create the sdist
2. Extract the sdist to a temporary location  
3. Change the working directory to the extracted sdist
4. Call build_wheel() from there

Alternatively, some build backends might support a custom hook for this, but that's not standardized.

Let me check if there are examples in the wild of how other tools accomplish this...

Actually, let me re-read the pyproject-hooks documentation. Maybe there's a way to pass the sdist path somehow.

Wait, let me check the build package source to see how they do it.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me check if there are any examples or references to this pattern. Now let me create a minimal reproduction script to understand and test the current behavior:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pdm-project__pdm__2.15/reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Script to reproduce the issue with building wheel from sdist."""

import os
import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path

def create_cython_project():
    """Create a test project with Cython files that should be included in sdist."""
    # Create a temporary directory for our test project
    project_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="pdm_cython_test_"))
    print(f"Creating test project in: {project_dir}")
    
    # Create pyproject.toml
    pyproject_content = """[build-system]
requires = ["setuptools", "wheel"]
build-backend = "setuptools.build_meta"

[project]
name = "test-cython-project"
version = "0.1.0"
description = "Test project with Cython files"
"""
    (project_dir / "pyproject.toml").write_text(pyproject_content)
    
    # Create a Python module
    (project_dir / "test_module.py").write_text("""def hello():
    return "Hello from Python!"
""")
    
    # Create a Cython file (.pyx) that should be included in sdist
    (project_dir / "cython_module.pyx").write_text("""def cython_hello():
    return "Hello from Cython!"
""")
    
    # Create setup.py for setuptools to know about the Cython file
    setup_content = """from setuptools import setup, Extension

setup(
    name="test-cython-project",
    version="0.1.0",
    py_modules=["test_module"],
    ext_modules=[
        Extension(
            "cython_module",
            ["cython_module.pyx"],  # This requires the .pyx file to be present
        )
    ],
    zip_safe=False,
)
"""
    (project_dir / "setup.py").write_text(setup_content)
    
    return project_dir

def test_current_behavior():
    """Test the current PDM build behavior."""
    print("=== Testing Current PDM Build Behavior ===")
    
    project_dir = create_cython_project()
    
    try:
        # Change to project directory
        original_cwd = os.getcwd()
        os.chdir(project_dir)
        
        # Try to build with PDM
        print("Building with PDM...")
        result = os.system("pdm build")
        
        if result == 0:
            print("✓ PDM build succeeded")
            
            # Check what was created
            dist_dir = project_dir / "dist"
            if dist_dir.exists():
                files = list(dist_dir.iterdir())
                print(f"Created files: {[f.name for f in files]}")
                
                # Check sdist contents
                sdist_files = [f for f in files if f.name.endswith(".tar.gz")]
                if sdist_files:
                    import tarfile
                    with tarfile.open(sdist_files[0], "r:gz") as tar:
                        members = tar.getnames()
                        pyx_files = [m for m in members if m.endswith(".pyx")]
                        print(f"Files in sdist: {len(members)} total")
                        print(f"Pyx files in sdist: {pyx_files}")
                        
                        if not pyx_files:
                            print("❌ ISSUE REPRODUCED: .pyx files missing from sdist!")
                        else:
                            print("✓ .pyx files correctly included in sdist")
                else:
                    print("No sdist found")
                    
        else:
            print("❌ PDM build failed")
            
    finally:
        os.chdir(original_cwd)
        # Clean up
        shutil.rmtree(project_dir)

"""Simple script to understand the build process."""

import os
import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path

def create_simple_project():
    """Create a simple test project.""" 
    # Create a temporary directory for our test project
    project_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="pdm_simple_test_"))
    print(f"Creating test project in: {project_dir}")
    
    # Create pyproject.toml with proper Python version constraint
    pyproject_content = """[build-system]
requires = ["setuptools>=40.8.0", "wheel"]
build-backend = "setuptools.build_meta"

[project]
name = "test-simple-project"
version = "0.1.0"
description = "Test simple project"
requires-python = ">=3.7"
"""
    (project_dir / "pyproject.toml").write_text(pyproject_content)
    
    # Create a Python module
    (project_dir / "test_module.py").write_text("""def hello():
    return "Hello from Python!"
""")
    
    # Create setup.py 
    setup_content = """from setuptools import setup

setup(
    name="test-simple-project",
    version="0.1.0", 
    py_modules=["test_module"],
)
"""
    (project_dir / "setup.py").write_text(setup_content)
    
    return project_dir

def test_simple_build():
    """Test simple build to understand current behavior."""
    print("=== Testing Simple PDM Build ===")
    
    project_dir = create_simple_project()
    
    try:
        # Change to project directory
        original_cwd = os.getcwd()
        os.chdir(project_dir)
        
        # Try to build with PDM
        print("Building with PDM...")
        result = os.system("pdm build")
        
        if result == 0:
            print("✓ PDM build succeeded")
            
            # Check what was created
            dist_dir = project_dir / "dist"
            if dist_dir.exists():
                files = list(dist_dir.iterdir())
                print(f"Created files: {[f.name for f in files]}")
                
                # Check sdist contents
                sdist_files = [f for f in files if f.name.endswith(".tar.gz")]
                if sdist_files:
                    import tarfile
                    with tarfile.open(sdist_files[0], "r:gz") as tar:
                        members = tar.getnames()
                        print(f"Files in sdist: {len(members)} total")
                        for member in sorted(members)[:10]:  # Show first 10
                            print(f"  {member}")
                            
        else:
            print("❌ PDM build failed")
            
    finally:
        os.chdir(original_cwd)
        # Clean up
        shutil.rmtree(project_dir)

if __name__ == "__main__":

"""Test script to reproduce the Cython sdist issue."""

import os
import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
import tarfile

def create_cython_project_with_missing_files():
    """Create a project that demonstrates the issue: .pyx files not included in sdist."""
    project_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="pdm_cython_issue_"))
    print(f"Creating test project in: {project_dir}")
    
    # Create pyproject.toml with proper constraints
    pyproject_content = """[build-system]
requires = ["setuptools>=40.8.0", "wheel"]
build-backend = "setuptools.build_meta"

[project]
name = "test-cython-issue"
version = "0.1.0"
description = "Test project showing Cython sdist issue"
requires-python = ">=3.7"
"""
    (project_dir / "pyproject.toml").write_text(pyproject_content)
    
    # Create a regular Python module
    (project_dir / "test_module.py").write_text("""def hello():
    return "Hello from Python!"
""")
    
    # Create a Cython file that SHOULD be included in sdist but won't be by default
    (project_dir / "cython_ext.pyx").write_text("""def cython_func():
    return "Hello from Cython!"
""")
    
    # Create setup.py that expects the .pyx file to be present
    setup_content = """from setuptools import setup, Extension

# This setup requires cython_ext.pyx to be present for the extension to build
setup(
    name="test-cython-issue",
    version="0.1.0",
    py_modules=["test_module"],
    ext_modules=[
        Extension(
            "cython_ext",
            ["cython_ext.pyx"],  # This will fail if .pyx is not in sdist
        )
    ],
)
"""
    (project_dir / "setup.py").write_text(setup_content)
    
    return project_dir

def test_current_behavior():
    """Test the current PDM build behavior - both sdist and wheel succeed independently."""
    print("=== Testing Current PDM Build Behavior (Issue Demonstration) ===")
    
    project_dir = create_cython_project_with_missing_files()
    
    try:
        original_cwd = os.getcwd()
        os.chdir(project_dir)
        
        print("Building with PDM (both sdist and wheel)...")
        result = os.system("pdm build")
        
        if result == 0:
            print("✓ PDM build succeeded (BUT THIS IS THE PROBLEM!)")
            
            # Check what was created
            dist_dir = project_dir / "dist"
            if dist_dir.exists():
                files = list(dist_dir.iterdir())
                print(f"Created files: {[f.name for f in files]}")
                
                # Check sdist contents
                sdist_files = [f for f in files if f.name.endswith(".tar.gz")]
                if sdist_files:
                    with tarfile.open(sdist_files[0], "r:gz") as tar:
                        members = tar.getnames()
                        pyx_files = [m for m in members if m.endswith(".pyx")]
                        print(f"Files in sdist: {len(members)} total")
                        print(f"Pyx files in sdist: {pyx_files}")
                        
                        if not pyx_files:
                            print("❌ ISSUE CONFIRMED: .pyx files missing from sdist!")
                            print("   But the build still succeeded, which is the problem.")
                            print("   If someone tries to install from this sdist, it will fail.")
                        else:
                            print("✓ .pyx files correctly included in sdist")
                            
        else:
            print("❌ PDM build failed (this might indicate the fix is working)")
            
    finally:
        os.chdir(original_cwd)
        shutil.rmtree(project_dir)

def test_manual_sdist_then_wheel():
    """Test what happens if we manually build sdist then try to build wheel from it."""
    print("\n=== Testing Manual SDist Then Wheel Process ===")
    
    project_dir = create_cython_project_with_missing_files()
    
    try:
        original_cwd = os.getcwd()
        os.chdir(project_dir)
        
        # First build only sdist
        print("Building only sdist...")
        result_sdist = os.system("pdm build --no-wheel")
        
        if result_sdist == 0:
            print("✓ SDist build succeeded")
            
            # Check sdist contents
            dist_dir = project_dir / "dist"
            sdist_files = list(dist_dir.glob("*.tar.gz"))
            if sdist_files:
                with tarfile.open(sdist_files[0], "r:gz") as tar:
                    members = tar.getnames()
                    pyx_files = [m for m in members if m.endswith(".pyx")]
                    print(f"Pyx files in sdist: {pyx_files}")
                    
                    if not pyx_files:
                        print("❌ As expected, .pyx files missing from sdist")
                        print("   Now let's see what happens when we try to build wheel...")
                        
                        # Try to extract sdist and build wheel from it
                        import tempfile
                        extract_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())
                        try:
                            tar.extractall(path=extract_dir)
                            extracted_project = list(extract_dir.iterdir())[0]  # Get the extracted folder
                            print(f"Extracted to: {extracted_project}")
                            
                            # Try to build wheel from extracted sdist
                            os.chdir(extracted_project)
                            print("Attempting to build wheel from extracted sdist...")
                            result_wheel = os.system("python -m pip wheel . --no-deps -w ../wheel_dist")
                            
                            if result_wheel != 0:
                                print("✅ EXPECTED FAILURE: Wheel build from incomplete sdist failed!")
                                print("   This demonstrates WHY we need to build wheel from sdist.")
                            else:
                                print("❌ Unexpected: Wheel build succeeded even with missing files")
                                
                        finally:
                            shutil.rmtree(extract_dir)
                            
        else:
            print("❌ SDist build failed")
            
    finally:
        os.chdir(original_cwd)
        shutil.rmtree(project_dir)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_current_behavior()

"""Test script to reproduce the Cython sdist issue by explicitly excluding .pyx files."""

import os
import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
import tarfile

def create_cython_project_with_exclusion():
    """Create a project where .pyx files are explicitly not included in sdist."""
    project_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="pdm_cython_exclusion_"))
    print(f"Creating test project in: {project_dir}")
    
    # Create pyproject.toml with proper constraints
    pyproject_content = """[build-system]
requires = ["setuptools>=40.8.0", "wheel"]
build-backend = "setuptools.build_meta"

[project]
name = "test-cython-exclusion"
version = "0.1.0"
description = "Test project with explicit exclusion"
requires-python = ">=3.7"
"""
    (project_dir / "pyproject.toml").write_text(pyproject_content)
    
    # Create a regular Python module
    (project_dir / "test_module.py").write_text("""def hello():
    return "Hello from Python!"
""")
    
    # Create a Cython file that we'll explicitly exclude from sdist
    (project_dir / "cython_ext.pyx").write_text("""def cython_func():
    return "Hello from Cython!"
""")
    
    # Create MANIFEST.in to explicitly exclude .pyx files from sdist
    manifest_content = """global-exclude *.pyx
include *.py
include pyproject.toml
include setup.py
"""
    (project_dir / "MANIFEST.in").write_text(manifest_content)
    
    # Create setup.py that expects the .pyx file to be present
    setup_content = """from setuptools import setup, Extension

# This setup requires cython_ext.pyx to be present for the extension to build
setup(
    name="test-cython-exclusion",
    version="0.1.0",
    py_modules=["test_module"],
    ext_modules=[
        Extension(
            "cython_ext",
            ["cython_ext.pyx"],  # This will fail if .pyx is not in sdist
        )
    ],
)
"""
    (project_dir / "setup.py").write_text(setup_content)
    
    return project_dir

def test_exclusion_behavior():
    """Test the current PDM build behavior with explicit exclusion."""
    print("=== Testing PDM Build Behavior with Explicit .pyx Exclusion ===")
    
    project_dir = create_cython_project_with_exclusion()
    
    try:
        original_cwd = os.getcwd()
        os.chdir(project_dir)
        
        print("Building with PDM (both sdist and wheel)...")
        result = os.system("pdm build")
        
        if result == 0:
            print("✓ PDM build succeeded (BUT THIS IS THE PROBLEM!)")
            
            # Check what was created
            dist_dir = project_dir / "dist"
            if dist_dir.exists():
                files = list(dist_dir.iterdir())
                print(f"Created files: {[f.name for f in files]}")
                
                # Check sdist contents
                sdist_files = [f for f in files if f.name.endswith(".tar.gz")]
                if sdist_files:
                    with tarfile.open(sdist_files[0], "r:gz") as tar:
                        members = tar.getnames()
                        pyx_files = [m for m in members if m.endswith(".pyx")]
                        py_files = [m for m in members if m.endswith(".py")]
                        print(f"Files in sdist: {len(members)} total")
                        print(f"Pyx files in sdist: {pyx_files}")
                        print(f"Py files in sdist: {len(py_files)}")
                        
                        if not pyx_files:
                            print("❌ ISSUE CONFIRMED: .pyx files explicitly excluded from sdist!")
                            print("   But the build still succeeded, which is the problem.")
                            print("   If someone tries to install from this sdist, it will fail.")
                        else:
                            print("✓ .pyx files incorrectly included in sdist despite exclusion")
                            
        else:
            print("❌ PDM build failed")
            
    finally:
        os.chdir(original_cwd)
        shutil.rmtree(project_dir)

def test_manual_process():
    """Test manual sdist then wheel to show the difference."""
    print("\n=== Testing Manual SDist Then Wheel Process ===")
    
    project_dir = create_cython_project_with_exclusion()
    
    try:
        original_cwd = os.getcwd()
        os.chdir(project_dir)
        
        # First build only sdist
        print("Building only sdist...")
        result_sdist = os.system("pdm build --no-wheel")
        
        if result_sdist == 0:
            print("✓ SDist build succeeded")
            
            # Check sdist contents
            dist_dir = project_dir / "dist"
            sdist_files = list(dist_dir.glob("*.tar.gz"))
            if sdist_files:
                with tarfile.open(sdist_files[0], "r:gz") as tar:
                    members = tar.getnames()
                    pyx_files = [m for m in members if m.endswith(".pyx")]
                    print(f"Pyx files in sdist: {pyx_files}")
                    
                    if not pyx_files:
                        print("❌ As expected, .pyx files excluded from sdist")
                        print("   Now let's see what happens when we try to build wheel...")
                        
                        # Try to extract sdist and build wheel from it
                        import tempfile
                        extract_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())
                        try:
                            tar.extractall(path=extract_dir)
                            extracted_projects = list(extract_dir.iterdir())
                            if extracted_projects:
                                extracted_project = extracted_projects[0]  # Get the extracted folder
                                print(f"Extracted to: {extracted_project}")
                                
                                # Try to build wheel from extracted sdist
                                os.chdir(extracted_project)
                                print("Attempting to build wheel from extracted sdist...")
                                result_wheel = os.system("python -m pip wheel . --no-deps -w ../wheel_dist")
                                
                                if result_wheel != 0:
                                    print("✅ EXPECTED FAILURE: Wheel build from incomplete sdist failed!")
                                    print("   This demonstrates WHY we need to build wheel from sdist.")
                                else:
                                    print("❌ Unexpected: Wheel build succeeded even with missing files")
                                    
                        finally:
                            shutil.rmtree(extract_dir)
                            
        else:
            print("❌ SDist build failed")
            
    finally:
        os.chdir(original_cwd)
        shutil.rmtree(project_dir)

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I understand the problem clearly:

**Problem**: PDM builds sdist and wheel independently. This means:
1. SDist gets built from source, potentially missing files due to MANIFEST.in exclusions
2. Wheel gets built from the original source directory, which has all files
3. Both succeed, but the sdist is actually broken

**Location**: The problem is in `/workspace/pdm-project__pdm__2.15/src/pdm/cli/commands/build.py` in the `do_build` method (lines 58-67).

**How test reproduces it**: My test creates a MANIFEST.in that excludes .pyx files, so the sdist doesn't contain them, but the wheel build succeeds because it uses the original source.

**Best practices for the fix**:
1. Minimal changes to existing functionality
2. Backward compatibility - don't break existing projects
3. Follow the same approach as the `build` package
4. Handle errors gracefully

**How to fix**:
1. When both sdist and wheel are requested, build sdist first
2. Extract the sdist to a temporary directory  
3. Build the wheel from the extracted sdist directory instead of original source
4. This ensures that if sdist is missing files, wheel build will fail too

The challenge is that the pyproject-hooks API doesn't have a direct "build_wheel_from_sdist" method. I need to:
1. Create a WheelBuilder that operates on the extracted sdist directory instead of original source
2. Or see if there's a way to make the existing WheelBuilder work with a different source directory

Looking at the builders, they take `src_dir` in their constructor. So I could:
1. Build sdist first 
2. Extract sdist to temp directory
3. Create a new WheelBuilder with the extracted directory as src_dir
4. Build wheel from that

This seems like the cleanest approach.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The current PDM build process builds sdist and wheel independently. This means if the sdist is missing required files (like .pyx files due to MANIFEST.in exclusions), the build will still succeed because the wheel is built from the original source directory rather than validating the sdist.

### 5.2 Problem Location  
The issue is in `/workspace/pdm-project__pdm__2.15/src/pdm/cli/commands/build.py` in the `do_build` method (lines 58-67), where sdist and wheel are built in sequence but independently.

### 5.3 How Test Reproduces Issue
My test creates a MANIFEST.in that excludes .pyx files, causing the sdist to be incomplete. When manually extracting the sdist and trying to build a wheel from it, the build fails because the .pyx files are missing. However, PDM's current approach succeeds because it builds the wheel from the original source.

### 5.4 Best Practices for Fix
- Make minimal changes to preserve backward compatibility
- Follow the same approach as the established `build` package  
- Handle errors gracefully with proper error messages
- Ensure the fix works for all existing use cases

### 5.5 Solution Approach
Modify the build process to:
1. When both sdist and wheel are requested, build sdist first
2. Extract the sdist to a temporary directory
3. Build the wheel from the extracted sdist directory (not original source)
4. This validates that the sdist contains all necessary files

Implementation requires creating a WheelBuilder with the extracted sdist as the source directory.

I'll modify the `do_build` method to build wheel from sdist when both are requested:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pdm-project__pdm__2.15/src/pdm/cli/commands/build.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    @staticmethod
    def do_build(
        project: Project,
        sdist: bool = True,
        wheel: bool = True,
        dest: str = "dist",
        clean: bool = True,
        config_settings: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
        hooks: HookManager | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        """Build artifacts for distribution."""
        from pdm.builders import SdistBuilder, WheelBuilder

        hooks = hooks or HookManager(project)
        config_settings = project.core.state.config_settings

        if project.is_global:
            raise ProjectError("Not allowed to build based on the global project.")
        if not project.is_distribution:  # pragma: no cover
            raise ProjectError("tool.pdm.distribution must be `true` to be built.")
        if not wheel and not sdist:
            project.core.ui.echo("All artifacts are disabled, nothing to do.", err=True)
            return
        if not os.path.isabs(dest):
            dest = project.root.joinpath(dest).as_posix()
        if clean:
            shutil.rmtree(dest, ignore_errors=True)
        if not os.path.exists(dest):
            os.makedirs(dest, exist_ok=True)
        hooks.try_emit("pre_build", dest=dest, config_settings=config_settings)
        artifacts: list[str] = []
        with project.core.ui.logging("build"):
            if sdist:
                project.core.ui.echo("Building sdist...")
                loc = SdistBuilder(project.root, project.environment).build(dest)
                project.core.ui.echo(f"Built sdist at {loc}")
                artifacts.append(loc)
            if wheel:
                project.core.ui.echo("Building wheel...")
                loc = WheelBuilder(project.root, project.environment).build(dest)
                project.core.ui.echo(f"Built wheel at {loc}")
                artifacts.append(loc)
        hooks.try_emit("post_build", artifacts=artifacts, config_settings=config_settings)</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    @staticmethod
    def do_build(
        project: Project,
        sdist: bool = True,
        wheel: bool = True,
        dest: str = "dist",
        clean: bool = True,
        config_settings: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
        hooks: HookManager | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        """Build artifacts for distribution."""
        from pdm.builders import SdistBuilder, WheelBuilder

        hooks = hooks or HookManager(project)
        config_settings = project.core.state.config_settings

        if project.is_global:
            raise ProjectError("Not allowed to build based on the global project.")
        if not project.is_distribution:  # pragma: no cover
            raise ProjectError("tool.pdm.distribution must be `true` to be built.")
        if not wheel and not sdist:
            project.core.ui.echo("All artifacts are disabled, nothing to do.", err=True)
            return
        if not os.path.isabs(dest):
            dest = project.root.joinpath(dest).as_posix()
        if clean:
            shutil.rmtree(dest, ignore_errors=True)
        if not os.path.exists(dest):
            os.makedirs(dest, exist_ok=True)
        hooks.try_emit("pre_build", dest=dest, config_settings=config_settings)
        artifacts: list[str] = []
        with project.core.ui.logging("build"):
            sdist_location = None
            if sdist:
                project.core.ui.echo("Building sdist...")
                sdist_location = SdistBuilder(project.root, project.environment).build(dest)
                project.core.ui.echo(f"Built sdist at {sdist_location}")
                artifacts.append(sdist_location)
            
            if wheel:
                project.core.ui.echo("Building wheel...")
                if sdist and sdist_location:
                    # Build wheel from sdist to ensure sdist is valid
                    wheel_location = Command._build_wheel_from_sdist(
                        project, sdist_location, dest, artifacts
                    )
                else:
                    # Build wheel normally (from source)
                    wheel_location = WheelBuilder(project.root, project.environment).build(dest)
                project.core.ui.echo(f"Built wheel at {wheel_location}")
                artifacts.append(wheel_location)
        hooks.try_emit("post_build", artifacts=artifacts, config_settings=config_settings)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/pdm-project__pdm__2.15/src/pdm/cli/commands/build.py has been edited.     @staticmethod
    def _build_wheel_from_sdist(
        project: Project, sdist_path: str, dest: str, artifacts: list[str]
    ) -> str:
        """Build wheel from sdist to ensure sdist contains all required files."""
        import tarfile
        import tempfile
        from pathlib import Path
        from pdm.builders import WheelBuilder
        
        # Extract sdist to temporary directory
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="pdm-build-") as temp_dir:
            extract_path = Path(temp_dir)
            try:
                with tarfile.open(sdist_path, "r:gz") as tar:
                    tar.extractall(path=extract_path)
            except Exception as e:
                raise BuildError(f"Failed to extract sdist {sdist_path}: {e}")
            
            # Find the extracted project directory (should be the only directory)
            extracted_items = list(extract_path.iterdir())
            if len(extracted_items) != 1 or not extracted_items[0].is_dir():
                raise BuildError(f"Unexpected sdist extraction result in {extract_path}")
            
            extracted_project_dir = extracted_items[0]
            
            # Build wheel from extracted sdist
            try:
                wheel_builder = WheelBuilder(extracted_project_dir, project.environment)
                return wheel_builder.build(dest)
            except Exception as e:
                raise BuildError(f"Failed to build wheel from sdist: {e}")</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>        hooks.try_emit("post_build", artifacts=artifacts, config_settings=config_settings)</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/pdm-project__pdm__2.15/src/pdm/cli/commands/build.py has been edited.     75	                project.core.ui.echo(f"Built wheel at {wheel_location}")
    76	                artifacts.append(wheel_location)
    77	        hooks.try_emit("post_build", artifacts=artifacts, config_settings=config_settings)

     1	from __future__ import annotations
     2	
     3	import argparse
     4	import os
     5	import shutil
     6	from typing import Mapping
     7	
     8	from pdm.cli.commands.base import BaseCommand
     9	from pdm.cli.hooks import HookManager
    10	from pdm.cli.options import (
    11	    config_setting_option,
    12	    no_isolation_option,
    13	    project_option,
    14	    skip_option,
    15	    verbose_option,
    16	)
    17	from pdm.exceptions import BuildError, ProjectError
    18	from pdm.project import Project
    19	
    20	
    21	class Command(BaseCommand):
    22	    """Build artifacts for distribution"""
    23	
    24	    arguments = (verbose_option, project_option, no_isolation_option, skip_option, config_setting_option)
    25	
    26	    @staticmethod
    27	    def do_build(
    28	        project: Project,
    29	        sdist: bool = True,
    30	        wheel: bool = True,
    31	        dest: str = "dist",
    32	        clean: bool = True,
    33	        config_settings: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
    34	        hooks: HookManager | None = None,
    35	    ) -> None:
    36	        """Build artifacts for distribution."""
    37	        from pdm.builders import SdistBuilder, WheelBuilder
    38	
    39	        hooks = hooks or HookManager(project)
    40	        config_settings = project.core.state.config_settings
    41	
    42	        if project.is_global:
    43	            raise ProjectError("Not allowed to build based on the global project.")
    44	        if not project.is_distribution:  # pragma: no cover
    45	            raise ProjectError("tool.pdm.distribution must be `true` to be built.")
    46	        if not wheel and not sdist:
    47	            project.core.ui.echo("All artifacts are disabled, nothing to do.", err=True)
    48	            return
    49	        if not os.path.isabs(dest):
    50	            dest = project.root.joinpath(dest).as_posix()
    51	        if clean:
    52	            shutil.rmtree(dest, ignore_errors=True)
    53	        if not os.path.exists(dest):
    54	            os.makedirs(dest, exist_ok=True)
    55	        hooks.try_emit("pre_build", dest=dest, config_settings=config_settings)
    56	        artifacts: list[str] = []
    57	        with project.core.ui.logging("build"):
    58	            sdist_location = None
    59	            if sdist:
    60	                project.core.ui.echo("Building sdist...")
    61	                sdist_location = SdistBuilder(project.root, project.environment).build(dest)
    62	                project.core.ui.echo(f"Built sdist at {sdist_location}")
    63	                artifacts.append(sdist_location)
    64	            
    65	            if wheel:
    66	                project.core.ui.echo("Building wheel...")
    67	                if sdist and sdist_location:
    68	                    # Build wheel from sdist to ensure sdist is valid
    69	                    wheel_location = Command._build_wheel_from_sdist(
    70	                        project, sdist_location, dest, artifacts
    71	                    )
    72	                else:
    73	                    # Build wheel normally (from source)
    74	                    wheel_location = WheelBuilder(project.root, project.environment).build(dest)
    75	                project.core.ui.echo(f"Built wheel at {wheel_location}")
    76	                artifacts.append(wheel_location)
    77	        hooks.try_emit("post_build", artifacts=artifacts, config_settings=config_settings)
    78	
    79	    @staticmethod
    80	    def _build_wheel_from_sdist(
    81	        project: Project, sdist_path: str, dest: str, artifacts: list[str]
    82	    ) -> str:
    83	        """Build wheel from sdist to ensure sdist contains all required files."""
    84	        import tarfile
    85	        import tempfile
    86	        from pathlib import Path
    87	        from pdm.builders import WheelBuilder
    88	        
    89	        # Extract sdist to temporary directory
    90	        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="pdm-build-") as temp_dir:
    91	            extract_path = Path(temp_dir)
    92	            try:
    93	                with tarfile.open(sdist_path, "r:gz") as tar:
    94	                    tar.extractall(path=extract_path)
    95	            except Exception as e:
    96	                raise BuildError(f"Failed to extract sdist {sdist_path}: {e}")
    97	            
    98	            # Find the extracted project directory (should be the only directory)
    99	            extracted_items = list(extract_path.iterdir())
   100	            if len(extracted_items) != 1 or not extracted_items[0].is_dir():
   101	                raise BuildError(f"Unexpected sdist extraction result in {extract_path}")
   102	            
   103	            extracted_project_dir = extracted_items[0]
   104	            
   105	            # Build wheel from extracted sdist
   106	            try:
   107	                wheel_builder = WheelBuilder(extracted_project_dir, project.environment)
   108	                return wheel_builder.build(dest)
   109	            except Exception as e:
   110	                raise BuildError(f"Failed to build wheel from sdist: {e}")
   111	
   112	    def add_arguments(self, parser: argparse.ArgumentParser) -> None:
   113	        parser.add_argument(
   114	            "--no-sdist",
   115	            dest="sdist",
   116	            default=True,
   117	            action="store_false",
   118	            help="Don't build source tarballs",
   119	        )
   120	        parser.add_argument(
   121	            "--no-wheel",
   122	            dest="wheel",
   123	            default=True,
   124	            action="store_false",
   125	            help="Don't build wheels",
   126	        )
   127	        parser.add_argument("-d", "--dest", default="dist", help="Target directory to put artifacts")
   128	        parser.add_argument(
   129	            "--no-clean",
   130	            dest="clean",
   131	            default=True,
   132	            action="store_false",
   133	            help="Do not clean the target directory",
   134	        )
   135	
   136	    def handle(self, project: Project, options: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   137	        self.do_build(
   138	            project,
   139	            sdist=options.sdist,
   140	            wheel=options.wheel,
   141	            dest=options.dest,
   142	            clean=options.clean,
   143	            hooks=HookManager(project, options.skip),
   144	        )

import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
import os

# Create a test project with the same issue
project_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='pdm_fix_test_'))
print(f'Creating test in: {project_dir}')

# Create files
(project_dir / 'pyproject.toml').write_text('''[build-system]
requires = [\"setuptools>=40.8.0\", \"wheel\"]
build-backend = \"setuptools.build_meta\"

[project]
name = \"test-fix\"
version = \"0.1.0\"
requires-python = \">=3.7\"
''')

(project_dir / 'test_module.py').write_text('def hello(): return \"Hello\"')

(project_dir / 'cython_ext.pyx').write_text('def cython_func(): return \"Cython\"')

(project_dir / 'MANIFEST.in').write_text('global-exclude *.pyx')

(project_dir / 'setup.py').write_text('''from setuptools import setup, Extension
setup(
    name=\"test-fix\",
    version=\"0.1.0\",
    py_modules=[\"test_module\"],
    ext_modules=[Extension(\"cython_ext\", [\"cython_ext.pyx\"])],
)
''')

# Test the build
original_cwd = os.getcwd()
try:
    os.chdir(project_dir)
    result = os.system('pdm build')
    if result != 0:
        print('✅ SUCCESS: Build correctly failed due to missing .pyx files in sdist!')
    else:
        print('❌ FAILURE: Build should have failed but succeeded')
finally:
    os.chdir(original_cwd)
    shutil.rmtree(project_dir)

import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
import os
# Create a test project with the same issue
project_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='pdm_fix_test_'))
print(f'Creating test in: {project_dir}')
# Create files
(project_dir / 'pyproject.toml').write_text('''[build-system]
requires = [\"setuptools>=40.8.0\", \"wheel\"]
build-backend = \"setuptools.build_meta\"
[project]
name = \"test-fix\"
version = \"0.1.0\"
requires-python = \">=3.7\"
''')
(project_dir / 'test_module.py').write_text('def hello(): return \"Hello\"')
(project_dir / 'cython_ext.pyx').write_text('def cython_func(): return \"Cython\"')
(project_dir / 'MANIFEST.in').write_text('global-exclude *.pyx')
(project_dir / 'setup.py').write_text('''from setuptools import setup, Extension
setup(
    name=\"test-fix\",
    version=\"0.1.0\",
    py_modules=[\"test_module\"],
    ext_modules=[Extension(\"cython_ext\", [\"cython_ext.pyx\"])],
)
''')
# Test the build
original_cwd = os.getcwd()
try:
    os.chdir(project_dir)
    result = os.system('pdm build')
    if result != 0:
        print('✅ SUCCESS: Build correctly failed due to missing .pyx files in sdist!')
    else:
        print('❌ FAILURE: Build should have failed but succeeded')
finally:
    os.chdir(original_cwd)
    shutil.rmtree(project_dir)

import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
import os

# Test 1: Only build sdist (--no-wheel)
print('=== Test 1: Only sdist ===')
project_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='pdm_sdist_only_'))
(project_dir / 'pyproject.toml').write_text('''[build-system]
requires = [\"setuptools>=40.8.0\", \"wheel\"]
build-backend = \"setuptools.build_meta\"
[project]
name = \"test-sdist-only\"
version = \"0.1.0\"
requires-python = \">=3.7\"
''')
(project_dir / 'test_module.py').write_text('def hello(): return \"Hello\"')

original_cwd = os.getcwd()
try:
    os.chdir(project_dir)
    result = os.system('pdm build --no-wheel')
    print('✅ SDist-only build succeeded' if result == 0 else '❌ SDist-only build failed')
finally:
    os.chdir(original_cwd)
    shutil.rmtree(project_dir)

# Test 2: Only build wheel (--no-sdist)
print('\\n=== Test 2: Only wheel ===')
project_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='pdm_wheel_only_'))

    def _build_wheel_from_sdist(
        project: Project, sdist_path: str, dest: str, artifacts: list[str]
    ) -> str:
        """Build wheel from sdist to ensure sdist contains all required files."""
        import tarfile
        import tempfile
        from pathlib import Path
        from pdm.builders import WheelBuilder
        
        # Extract sdist to temporary directory
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="pdm-build-") as temp_dir:
            extract_path = Path(temp_dir)
            try:
                with tarfile.open(sdist_path, "r:gz") as tar:
                    tar.extractall(path=extract_path)
            except Exception as e:
                raise BuildError(f"Failed to extract sdist {sdist_path}: {e}")
            
            # Find the extracted project directory (should be the only directory)
            extracted_items = list(extract_path.iterdir())
            if len(extracted_items) != 1 or not extracted_items[0].is_dir():
                raise BuildError(f"Unexpected sdist extraction result in {extract_path}")
            
            extracted_project_dir = extracted_items[0]
            
            # Build wheel from extracted sdist
            try:
                wheel_builder = WheelBuilder(extracted_project_dir, project.environment)
                return wheel_builder.build(dest)
            except Exception as e:
                raise BuildError(f"Failed to build wheel from sdist: {e}")</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    @staticmethod

[Command finished with exit code 1]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>I notice in the logs: `INFO     pdm.termui:base.py:187 Preparing environment(Non-isolated mode) for PEP 517 build...` which indicates that the isolation setting IS being respected (it says Non-isolated mode), but the build is still failing.

The issue is that even in non-isolated mode, when building sdist, setuptools is running setup.py in a subprocess that doesn't inherit the project's installed packages properly.

Actually, let me step back and think about this differently. The failing test might actually be exposing that my change is working correctly!

Let me understand what the test is really testing:
1. It creates a project with a setup.py that imports "first" 
2. It installs "first" into the project
3. It builds with --no-isolation (non-isolated mode)
4. Previously, this would succeed because wheel was built directly from source
5. With my change, sdist is built first, and that fails because setup.py can't import "first" even in non-isolated mode

But this reveals a real issue: the sdist is broken! If someone were to download and install from the sdist, it would fail because setup.py imports "first" but "first" is not declared as a build dependency.

So arguably, my change is CORRECT and the test expectation is WRONG. The test should expect the build to fail because the sdist is broken.

However, to maintain backward compatibility, I should probably make my change conditional - only build wheel from sdist in isolated mode, and keep the old behavior in non-isolated mode.

Let me check what the `build` tool does in this scenario.

Actually, let me first check if the original code would pass this test.
